# AI Media Scout — 2026-10-19

Today’s expanded review adds practical guides across AI collage, neural sound design, 2D puppetry, avatar motion, video editing, spatial reconstruction, mapping and fabrication. Earlier screened tools receive detailed installation and first-use guidance; additional eligible discoveries enter the library with full-profile work still pending. All platforms receive equal consideration, with documented support and unknown requirements labeled explicitly. Quality remains evidence-based: no tool was installed or run, and no stars or completed guide automatically grant a recommendation. OpenSuno’s current source notices fail the open-source criterion for its integrated workflow; its historical entry remains preserved with a current license warning. The library gains 49 distinct tools; 19 earlier screened entries become full guides. The complete retained catalog was scanned at the metadata level, followed by bounded manual metadata review and targeted primary-source reads beyond the automatic README shortlist, including a final pass after collection. This was not a manual eligibility review of every search result; raw unreviewed matches remain private. All 464 planned repository queries and 18 model tasks were attempted across 61 fields. Repository search outcomes: 464 successful, 0 partial and 0 failed; 109 reached result bounds. Model query failures: 0. Coverage is not exhaustive. All eight configured web ecosystems have researched findings. Some primary pages returned script shells or failed to load; these limitations are disclosed in the field and source-group notes. Model metadata is a discovery signal, not permission to publish unreviewed software. The collection records 118 warnings, including source retrieval and bounded-search disclosures retained in the report coverage details.

Documentation reviewed today. Tools are not hands-on tested unless explicitly stated. Requirements and performance remain source-specific.

## Coverage

| Field | Finding |
| --- | --- |
| Images & design | Current visual-style and restoration pages describe model-guided appearance and enlargement. Today’s collage, masking and sprite guides make their actual AI stage explicit; conventional effects are not relabeled AI. [Source 1](https://curryjung.github.io/VisualStylePrompt/) [Source 2](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) |
| Video, animation & film | Read motion-guided animation pages and current editor documentation. Completed OpenChatCut, Editkin and production-workspace guides; paid generation, local editing and successful output export are separate checks. [Source 1](https://wan-move.github.io/) [Source 2](https://animationai.github.io/AnimateAnything/) |
| Audio, music & voice | Read Neutone’s real-time model host and current PyPI metadata. Completed neural morphing, audio super-resolution and TTS guides. The PyPI HTML page returned a challenge; JSON metadata was available. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source 2](https://pypi.org/pypi/neutone-sdk/json) |
| 3D, reconstruction & assets | Reviewed neural surface, material-dynamics and procedural-assembly research. Completed video-to-world and learned SfM guides; FastGS remains held because its core inherited software notice is non-commercial. [Source 1](https://open3dvlab.github.io/NeuRodin/) [Source 2](https://xjay18.github.io/NeuMA_page/) [Source 3](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Browser tools & web media | Current NodeTool material describes agent-created editable media. Browser interfaces do not imply local model inference. Racik returned only a title, so that page supplies no working-app evidence. [Source 1](https://nodetool.ai/) [Source 2](https://image.solusikoding.com) |
| WebXR, VR & AR | Reviewed Meta IWSDK and Google XR Blocks AI prototyping descriptions. Completed the VRCLT translation guide with Windows, audio routing and optional headset requirements kept explicit. [Source 1](https://developers.meta.com/vr/blog/accelerate-vr-development-with-ai-and-immersive-web-sdk/) [Source 2](https://research.google/blog/vibe-coding-xr-accelerating-ai-xr-prototyping-with-xr-blocks-and-gemini/) |
| Computational art & creative coding | Reviewed neural self-similarity representations and Cool-Chic’s codec setup. New collage and agent-drawing tools span learned reconstruction and programmatically authored artwork; those mechanisms are distinguished. [Source 1](https://orange-opensource.github.io/Cool-Chic/getting_started/quickstart.html) [Source 2](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| Interactive, immersive & live media | Timeflux documents biosignal processing for interactive installations, and Mind Draw Play describes audiovisual brain/heart-signal work. The agent and scene-editor candidates add interaction possibilities without establishing installation readiness. [Source 1](https://timeflux.io/) [Source 2](https://www.neur0forest.art/mind-draw-play/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/VAST-AI/EdenLive) |
| 3D printing & generative CAD | Reviewed text-to-CAD and procedural assembly guidance. Completed Bambu Studio AI and print3d-tools workflows; a mesh or engraving export alone does not prove fit, strength or manufacturing readiness. [Source 1](https://www.texttocad.dev/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Games & production pipelines | Cambrian describes an experimental agent-driven native game engine; GardenDesigner describes scene generation. Completed SpriteLift’s video-to-sprite guide with alpha consistency and engine-import checks still pending. [Source 1](https://cambrianengine.com/) [Source 2](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Motion capture & character animation | Learning2Dance and SemGeoMo document audio- and context-conditioned motion. Today’s Rigby2D and VRMA Lab guides focus on editable rig and humanoid-motion workflows. [Source 1](https://verlab.github.io/Learning2Dance_CAG_2020/) [Source 2](https://4dvlab.github.io/project_page/semgeomo/) |
| Avatars, digital humans & lip sync | Reviewed contextual motion generation and the current GNM Studio documentation. GNM’s webcam face tracking is concrete AI use; optional head fitting and custom model/texture terms remain separate gaps. [Source 1](https://4dvlab.github.io/project_page/semgeomo/) |
| VFX, compositing & relighting | Lumen and the SyncLight model card describe neural relighting. Completed OBS AI Matting, separating learned alpha estimation from its conventional color/exposure matching; output quality remains unverified. [Source 1](https://lumen-relight.github.io/) [Source 2](https://huggingface.co/davidserra9/synclight) |
| Spatial audio & volumetric media | Neural acoustic fields show emitter/listener-dependent sound, while SoundSpaces supplies simulation for learning. Video-to-world and learned SfM guides disclose reconstruction artifacts and hardware uncertainty. [Source 1](https://sites.google.com/view/nafs-neurips2022) [Source 2](https://vision.cs.utexas.edu/projects/soundspaces2/) [Source 3](https://openfollow.app/docs/person-detection.html) |
| Photogrammetry, scanning & neural rendering | LenslessPiCam and E2VID describe learned reconstruction from specialized sensors. Completed wearable IMU/MediaPipe data capture, keeping approximate timestamp alignment distinct from hardware synchronization. [Source 1](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) [Source 2](https://rpg.ifi.uzh.ch/E2VID.html) |
| Editing, captions & post-production | Reviewed OpenFilm’s agent-authored film structure and LocalSR’s restoration workflow. Expanded existing entries into usable editing guides; GetVect’s optional Gemini enhancement uploads the working image. [Source 1](https://openfilm.dev/) [Source 2](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) |
| Vector graphics, illustration & textures | VFIG describes vision-language raster-to-SVG reconstruction; MuseDraw offers editable diagram examples. AITracer and GetVect guides keep neural segmentation or re-illustration separate from deterministic polygon/vector conversion. [Source 1](https://vfig-proj.github.io/) [Source 2](https://kanjieteam.github.io/MuseDraw/) |
| Typography, fonts & layout | Strike a Chord uses diffusion supervision for glyph motion. Text Behind adds a documented SAM2 masking route to layered poster typography; image masks still require visual inspection. [Source 1](https://strikeachordkt.github.io/strikeachord/) |
| Storyboarding, narrative & comics | OpenFilm describes films as editable web documents. Estúdio and Renku guides cover project/shot organization with external AI providers; no automated film was produced by this research run. [Source 1](https://openfilm.dev/) |
| Creative publishing & presentation | OpenPresentation documents its renderer/exporter requirements; MuseDraw demonstrates editable visual communication. localpress qualifies for screening through local AI captions and matting, with site mutations requiring review. [Source 1](https://www.openpresentation.org/docs/get-started) [Source 2](https://kanjieteam.github.io/MuseDraw/) |
| Photography, restoration & color | Reviewed LocalSR’s before/after context and lensless-imaging methods. Neural enhancement is an estimate and may alter detail; photographic source ownership and uploaded-image processing remain relevant. [Source 1](https://herrei.github.io/localsr/) [Source 2](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) [Source 3](https://huggingface.co/davidserra9/synclight) |
| Data art & scientific visualization | QGIS AI Agent and VFIG provide concrete map and figure workflows. The QGIS catalog led to AITracer; its segmentation uses the visible canvas, so zoom limits geometry detail. [Source 1](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/ai_agent/) [Source 2](https://vfig-proj.github.io/) |

A renamed SolidCraft entry was not duplicated. [Source 1](https://www.texttocad.dev/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |

Direct plugin-page fetch returned 403, while the web research tool could read the official page. [Source 1](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/tags/ai/?sort=-created_on) [Source 2](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/aitracer/) |

| AI cel animation and inbetweening | AnimateAnything describes area/strength-guided diffusion animation. ToonComposer returned only a title; that retrieval cannot establish current functionality. Rigging and sprite workflows remain distinct from cel inbetweening. [Source 1](https://lg-li.github.io/project/tooncomposer/) [Source 2](https://animationai.github.io/AnimateAnything/) |
| AI Foley and sound effects | LTX’s official guide describes video-conditioned Foley synthesis. AudioSR’s new guide concerns inferred high frequencies, not original sound recovery; downloaded-model terms remain separate. [Source 1](https://docs.ltx.io/open-source-model/feature-guides/audio/video-to-audio-foley) |
| Learned media codecs and neural delivery | Cool-Chic provides codec setup, while LenslessMic describes optical audio encryption/reconstruction. No codec or hardware performance claim was independently benchmarked in this run. [Source 1](https://orange-opensource.github.io/Cool-Chic/getting_started/quickstart.html) [Source 2](https://blinorot.github.io/projects/LenslessMic/) [Source 3](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI assisted sound data art and corpus instruments | Neutone’s model examples and the YOLO-sonification source support adjacent sound practices. The latter still lacks enough documented setup detail for a complete guide; sample-brain’s available path is rule-based with a missing optional embedding module. [Source 1](https://neutone.ai/fx) |
| AI biosignal art and neurointeractive media | Timeflux and Mind Draw Play describe acquisition and audiovisual feedback. The new motion-capture guide separates MediaPipe targets, IMU measurements and missing/timing-error behavior. [Source 1](https://timeflux.io/) [Source 2](https://www.neur0forest.art/mind-draw-play/) |
| Neural procedural material authoring | Material Apprentice describes generating procedural materials from expert demonstrations. Procedura assigns editable materials to generated assemblies; no new production validation was inferred. [Source 1](https://materialapprentice.github.io/) [Source 2](https://spatiaos.github.io/projects/procedura/) |
| Neural cameras and unconventional image capture | E2VID learns intensity reconstruction from event streams; LenslessPiCam documents sensor/mask and reconstruction requirements. These are specialized acquisition workflows, not ordinary camera-app compatibility. [Source 1](https://rpg.ifi.uzh.ch/E2VID.html) [Source 2](https://lensless.readthedocs.io/) |
| AI origami, kirigami and paper engineering | COrigami’s abstract describes AI crease generation under foldability constraints. A paper and examples alone do not clear implementation licensing or demonstrate a physically successful fold. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26299) |
| AI landscape, garden and environmental design | GardenDesigner describes agent-guided Jiangnan garden scenes for virtual media. The reviewed demonstration does not certify real landscape engineering or construction suitability. [Source 1](https://monad-cube.github.io/GardenDesigner/) |
| Neural mosaic and stained-glass media | Neural Collages uses self-similarity for image representation. Today’s collage tools address editable compositions, but traditional mosaics or fractal algorithms without a demonstrated AI stage stay outside scope. [Source 1](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |
| AI optical illusions and anamorphic media | Visual Anagrams and Factorized Diffusion show transformation- and viewing-dependent images. Their primary demos remain useful context; no unchanged tool was recycled as a new profile. [Source 1](https://dangeng.github.io/visual_anagrams/) [Source 2](https://dangeng.github.io/factorized_diffusion/) [Source 3](https://diffusionillusions.com/) |
| AI relief engraving and learned halftoning | Inverse-halftoning research describes learned image reconstruction. Completed CoinForge Studio with AI background removal and agent design; physical engraving settings and source artwork rights remain separate. [Source 1](https://houxianxu.github.io/project/inverse-halftoning) |
| Neural brushstroke painting and drawn media | Read compositional and stylized neural-painting research. A brushstroke result or research origin does not establish code eligibility; earlier license gaps are not silently cleared. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2309.03504) [Source 2](https://jiupinjia.github.io/neuralpainter/) |
| AI color direction and palette-based media | Neural Preset describes learned per-pixel color mapping. OBS light matching and ordinary palette arithmetic are conventional components even when connected to an AI workflow. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.13511) |
| AI collage, cut-paper composition and layered media | Read Neural Collage Transfer research and reviewed AI collage editors and composition skills. CollageRL has non-commercial software terms; CollageNet and collage-diffusion-ui lack retrieved licenses. Those research results do not establish eligibility. New eligible composition guides disclose source-art and model terms. [Source 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.02202) [Source 2](https://zymrael.github.io/self-similarity-prior/) |

## Search and review counts

61 creative fields; 464/464 repository queries attempted; 18/18 model-task queries attempted. 25637 distinct source candidates and 845 model leads. 48 detailed profiles and 20 additional screened discoveries. Source gaps: 0 failed repository queries, 0 partial repository queries, 109 bounded repository queries, 0 failed model-task queries and 0 web ecosystem gaps. Raw search candidates include duplicates of known tools, excluded projects and projects awaiting review; they are not verified recommendations.

## Research beyond GitHub

- **gitlab** · searched: Read the bounded diffusion and music API searches. AudioSR and LongCat mirrors led to completed upstream guides. Many music matches are conventional players, mirrors or deleted projects; no license inference was made from those search descriptions. [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=diffusion&simple=true&per_page=50) [Source](https://gitlab.com/api/v4/projects?search=music&simple=true&per_page=50)
- **codeberg** · searched: The first fifty diffusion results were available through the API. Followed Black Latents into its README and license: the app is CC BY-NC-SA 4.0, so it remains excluded despite concrete neural-audio relevance. Public page access failed in the web tool; the primary API supplied the evidence. [Source](https://codeberg.org/api/v1/repos/search?q=diffusion&limit=50) [Source](https://codeberg.org/martstilde/black-latents-latent-diffusion-app)
- **sourcehut** · searched: Read the neural-project listing. Results were mainly generic neural-network tutorials and security research; no additional clearly creative, license-reviewed tool was established. This was a bounded listing, not an exhaustive ecosystem crawl. [Source](https://sr.ht/projects?search=neural)
- **packages** · searched: Read current Neutone SDK and AudioSR PyPI JSON. The fastgs package is a different geospatial project, not FastGS Gaussian reconstruction; package names are not identity evidence. F-Droid supplied current SDAI platform distinctions. [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/neutone-sdk/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/audiosr/json) [Source](https://pypi.org/pypi/fastgs/json) [Source](https://f-droid.org/en/packages/com.shifthackz.aisdv1.app.foss/)
- **creative-plugins** · searched: Reviewed current QGIS listings, AITracer, OBS neural matting, ComfyUI audio nodes and the Neural Morphing VST3 bridge. Complete host, model and runtime requirements remain separate from each plugin grant. [Source](https://plugins.qgis.org/plugins/aitracer/) [Source](https://neutone.ai/fx) [Source](https://github.com/ale200x/obs-ai-matting) [Source](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing)
- **project-sites** · searched: Refreshed primary sites across all sixty-one fields and followed new Iki, OpenChatCut, modl, localpress and Renku sites. Some pages supplied only script shells; three initial URLs failed, including SyncLight TLS. Those failures are not working-demo evidence. [Source](https://zeikar.dev/iki) [Source](https://openchatcut.com) [Source](https://modl.run) [Source](https://localpress.griffen.codes) [Source](https://gorenku.com/download/)
- **research-code** · searched: Read collage, scene, neural-audio, capture and physical-media research. Reviewed complete source grants and relevant notices; OpenSuno’s bundled non-commercial code reopens its existing library license check, while FastGS’s earlier hold remains justified. [Source](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.02202) [Source](https://github.com/victorcampeanu/OpenSuno-YuE2/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md) [Source](https://github.com/fastgs/FastGS/blob/main/scene/__init__.py)
- **international** · searched: Read Chinese/English VRMA and image-tool documentation, Vietnamese speech tooling, Portuguese production tooling and the German INTEX case. Language, popularity and research origin are not quality labels. [Source](https://github.com/YuBan834/vrma-lab) [Source](https://github.com/BsMediaVN/studio-agent) [Source](https://github.com/Leca-pt/estudio) [Source](https://www.green-ai-hub.de/pilotprojekte/pilotprojekt-intex-gmbh)

## extern_ai_collage

Images & design · AI collage, cut-paper composition and layered media · AI-assisted textile and computational craft

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-19; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Investigate recurring colors and forms in a personal body of work and produce visual studies for later physical work.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-19. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The repository describes an artist experiment and includes sample source and generated images; those examples are not a general quality evaluation. [Source](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Create a Python environment, install the documented CUDA PyTorch stack and requirements, then prepare a rights-cleared image folder before training. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** The full file grants MIT for code/documentation and separately names CreativeML Open RAIL-M for SD1.5. Example artworks remain copyrighted; artist datasets and LoRA weights have separate terms. Local GPU time and model/dataset storage; no required paid inference service is described. [Source](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Reported settings and training times belong to one dataset. Floating fragments, softened details and color drift are documented; this is not evidence that every archive trains successfully.

### How it uses AI

Scripts train a Stable Diffusion 1.5 LoRA on an artist’s own sculptural and mixed-media archive, then generate variations. [Source](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

### Introduction

Scripts train a Stable Diffusion 1.5 LoRA on an artist’s own sculptural and mixed-media archive, then generate variations. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

### What it is good for

Investigate recurring colors and forms in a personal body of work and produce visual studies for later physical work. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

### Demo & examples

The repository describes an artist experiment and includes sample source and generated images; those examples are not a general quality evaluation. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

### Install

Create a Python environment, install the documented CUDA PyTorch stack and requirements, then prepare a rights-cleared image folder before training. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Create a Python environment, install the documented CUDA PyTorch stack and requirements, then prepare a rights-cleared image folder before training.

```sh
python3 -m venv venv
```


```sh
pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
python scripts/prepare_dataset.py --input /path/to/your/images --output ./my_dataset --token my_style --size 512
```


```sh
python scripts/train_lora.py --dataset ./my_dataset --output ./training_output --epochs 7 --rank 64 --lr 5e-5
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

1. Prepare images with a distinct trigger token.
2. Train a small evaluation run and save checkpoints.
3. Use generate_simple.py with the selected checkpoint and inspect color shifts and memorization.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

- **Hardware:** The author reports GTX 1660 Super with 6 GB VRAM, 512-pixel images, latent caching and float32. This is a recorded setup, not a universal minimum. RAM and total disk requirements are not specified.
- **Software:** The recorded environment uses torch 2.1.0+cu121, diffusers 0.24.0, transformers 4.36.0, PEFT 0.7.1 and other pinned dependencies.
- **Platforms:** CUDA training is documented. Supported OS versions and an equivalent Apple Silicon route are not established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** The full file grants MIT for code/documentation and separately names CreativeML Open RAIL-M for SD1.5. Example artworks remain copyrighted; artist datasets and LoRA weights have separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. The full file grants MIT for code/documentation and separately names CreativeML Open RAIL-M for SD1.5. Example artworks remain copyrighted; artist datasets and LoRA weights have separate terms.
- **Cost:** Local GPU time and model/dataset storage; no required paid inference service is described.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

### Limitations

Reported settings and training times belong to one dataset. Floating fragments, softened details and color drift are documented; this is not evidence that every archive trains successfully. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/Jinkieyz/extern_ai_collage/blob/master/README.txt)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. AI composition is supplied by the agent; cutting and rasterization are conventional operations. Source-license suitability and final typography still require review.

### How it uses AI

An agent skill and image-processing scripts let a language model source, cut, arrange and iteratively render layered collage artwork. [Source](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An agent skill and image-processing scripts let a language model source, cut, arrange and iteratively render layered collage artwork. [Source 1](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Make editable SVG/PNG album-cover or poster studies from your own imagery or suitably licensed archives. [Source 1](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows sample briefs and outputs. Its render-consistency and production-time statements are maintainer claims, not our tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install the repository’s Claude Code plugin or plain skill, then satisfy its documented local image-processing dependencies. [Source 1](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the repository’s Claude Code plugin or plain skill, then satisfy its documented local image-processing dependencies.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design.git
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

1. Specify subject, size, intended use and a local image folder or allowed archive.
2. Let the agent compose and revise a small piece.
3. Inspect the SVG/PNG, build script and per-image attribution record before reuse.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/polgarp/collage-design/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** The author estimates up to about 540 MB of intermediates plus 25–250 MB of sources for a large piece. CPU, RAM and GPU minimums are not specified.
- **Software:** Claude Code; ImageMagick, Inkscape, librsvg, fontconfig, Python, Pillow and NumPy. No local neural-model dependency is required by its processing scripts.
- **Platforms:** The documented shell-based host is Claude Code on a local machine; claude.ai and its desktop chat are not supported execution hosts. A complete OS matrix is not documented.

### License, model weights & costs

- **Weights:** Apache-2.0 code; the repository identifies upstream canvas-design attribution. Each source image/font and the language-model service retains separate terms.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows Apache-2.0. Apache-2.0 code; the repository identifies upstream canvas-design attribution. Each source image/font and the language-model service retains separate terms.
- **Cost:** Language-model usage plus local processing; the README estimates 100–150k tokens and 20–40 minutes per piece, which are workflow-specific observations.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Current 0.x schema changes can reject older models. The author explicitly says it is not production-ready and the editor is early.

### How it uses AI

An AI-agent plugin combines image-generated character layers with a deterministic auto-rigger and WebGL2 puppet runtime. [Source](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

### Introduction

An AI-agent plugin combines image-generated character layers with a deterministic auto-rigger and WebGL2 puppet runtime. [Source 1](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

### What it is good for

Prototype browser characters with editable JSON rigs, blink, gaze, mouth motion and spring-driven hair. [Source 1](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

### Demo & examples

Official playground/editor links and a generated-character GIF are provided; we did not run the editor or assess deformation quality. [Source 1](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

### Install

Use the published engine/format packages for playback, or the documented plugin/MCP route with a separate image generator for character creation. [Source 1](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the published engine/format packages for playback, or the documented plugin/MCP route with a separate image generator for character creation.

```sh
npm install @ikijs/engine @ikijs/format
```


```sh
pnpm install
```


```sh
pnpm build
```


```sh
pnpm playground
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

1. Start from a supplied character or role-named PNG layers.
2. Generate or adjust the rig, then inspect poses and canvas bounds.
3. Export a validated .iki model and integrate it with a host supplying gaze and speech parameters.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/zeikar/iki/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://zeikar.dev/iki/)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** TypeScript/JavaScript packages; pnpm for source development; WebGL2 browser; an MCP-capable AI host and image generator for the agent workflow.
- **Platforms:** Browser runtime is documented. Native OS versions, mobile browser coverage and host-specific installation support require separate checks.

### Install

Install the npm CLI and Playwright Chromium; add FFmpeg to PATH for video export. Still-image export does not require FFmpeg. [Source 1](https://github.com/AnayGarodia/sketchling/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://anaygarodia.github.io/sketchling/)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install the npm CLI and Playwright Chromium; add FFmpeg to PATH for video export. Still-image export does not require FFmpeg.

```sh
npm install -g sketchling
```


```sh
npx playwright install chromium
```


```sh
sketchling render scene.ts --out preview.png
```


```sh
sketchling render scene.ts --video preview.mp4 --fps 24
```

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/AnayGarodia/sketchling/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://anaygarodia.github.io/sketchling/)

3. Export a short video and inspect timing, text and audio before combining scenes.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. No packaged installer. Bridge does not start automatically with the plugin; VST cannot independently render the entire host track. The optional ONNX route requires separately exported model files.

### How it uses AI

A DAC neural-audio effect matches source sound against a palette of audio grains for timbral morphing. [Source](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A DAC neural-audio effect matches source sound against a palette of audio grains for timbral morphing. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Designing hybrid instrument textures and resynthesized sound effects in a DAW or standalone interface. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository provides curated Gradio examples and a plugin/standalone workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Use the documented demo branch with JUCE submodules. Start the Python bridge separately, then build the VST3/standalone target with the appropriate platform CMake generator. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Use the documented demo branch with JUCE submodules. Start the Python bridge separately, then build the VST3/standalone target with the appropriate platform CMake generator.

```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python -m pip install -r bridge/requirements.txt
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python bridge/server.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

1. Load the plugin in a VST3 host or open the standalone and add palette sounds.
2. Select Bridge Only, DAC and Palette Only; check backend status, adjust dry/wet and render a short HQ WAV or DAW bounce.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Local minimum RAM/VRAM/disk not documented. The hosted example specifies 4 CPUs and 8 GiB memory; this is not a universal plugin minimum.
- **Software:** Git; CMake 3.22+; C++17 compiler; Python 3.10–3.12 bridge; VST3 DAW if used as a plugin. Windows Visual Studio 2022, macOS Xcode tools, or Linux JUCE build dependencies.
- **Platforms:** Windows, macOS and Linux source-build instructions. Bridge supports CPU/CUDA; macOS currently uses CPU rather than MPS.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT project code; JUCE, DAC model files and the chosen DAW retain separate licenses.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project code; JUCE, DAC model files and the chosen DAW retain separate licenses.
- **Cost:** Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

No packaged installer. Bridge does not start automatically with the plugin; VST cannot independently render the entire host track. The optional ONNX route requires separately exported model files. [Source 1](https://github.com/manoskary/neural-morphing/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

OBS filter uses recurrent RVM neural matting to remove or blur webcam backgrounds, with conventional automatic exposure/color matching. [Source](https://github.com/ale200x/obs-ai-matting/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

OBS filter uses recurrent RVM neural matting to remove or blur webcam backgrounds, with conventional automatic exposure/color matching. [Source 1](https://github.com/ale200x/obs-ai-matting/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

```sh

```


```sh
cd stackcut
```


```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
```


```sh

```


```sh

```


```sh
stackcut doctor
```


```sh
stackcut draft screen.mp4 face.mov -o edit.yaml
```


```sh
stackcut render edit.yaml
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. 1. Activate the created virtual environment using the command appropriate for your shell before installing packages.
2. Record screen and face simultaneously with both microphones enabled; draft the YAML edit from those recordings.
3. Review captions, synchronization, cuts, zooms and any privacy masks; optionally let a configured agent revise the plan.
4. Render the 9:16 output and inspect the video and contact sheet, especially clipped words, face framing and blurred information.

### How it uses AI

A wireframe/storyboard editor shares an editable board with an external AI client through MCP. The model drafts screens and acts on human review notes; offline sketch recognition and tracing are conventional operations. [Source](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A wireframe/storyboard editor shares an editable board with an external AI client through MCP. The model drafts screens and acts on human review notes; offline sketch recognition and tracing are conventional operations. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Iterating on interface storyboards and specifications with an agent while preserving an editable, portable board and linked preview screens. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README shows a board, realistic previews and a draft/review/update sequence generated through real bridge calls. The depicted prompt is an example; neither independent reproduction nor usability quality was tested here. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

For manual editing, download the standalone HTML app. For AI use, obtain the documented axsquiggle.mjs release, install Node 18+ and run it; no npm dependency installation is required for that built artifact. Register the same script as an MCP server in a compatible external AI client. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. For manual editing, download the standalone HTML app. For AI use, obtain the documented axsquiggle.mjs release, install Node 18+ and run it; no npm dependency installation is required for that built artifact. Register the same script as an MCP server in a compatible external AI client.

```sh
node axsquiggle.mjs
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

1. Open the localhost address printed by the bridge, normally http://127.0.0.1:4747/. Connect your configured MCP-capable AI client.
2. Ask the agent to draft a small board, edit it yourself and leave notes around the relevant elements.
3. Tell the agent to retrieve the notes and revise the board; inspect its changes and use undo when appropriate.
4. Save the board as JSON, preview linked screens and export the needed visual or specification format.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Standalone browser HTML for manual use; Node 18+ for the local AI bridge and a separately configured MCP-capable model client. The bridge uses a local authentication token.
- **Platforms:** The Node bridge is documented for Windows, macOS and Linux. Mouse, touch and pen editing are described. No native mobile MCP runtime support is established.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT editor/bridge; external model access and supplied images retain separate terms. No model weights or hosted AI subscription are included.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT editor/bridge; external model access and supplied images retain separate terms. No model weights or hosted AI subscription are included.
- **Cost:** Standalone local editing is available without an app account; external AI client/provider costs are separate.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

The offline trace feature does not use AI. The outside agent retrieves board data and may receive attached images, so offline editing is distinct from external inference. Browser autosave is not a portable backup; save JSON. Images inserted into the board are scaled to 800 pixels. RAM, VRAM and installed storage minimums are undocumented. [Source 1](https://github.com/christaylor98/axSquiggle/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A local MCP bridge lets an external AI client read, modify and export CAD drawings through constrained editing operations. It adds visual feedback, input snapshots and transaction checks around an existing CAD installation. The source path instead requires Node 22.19+, PowerShell 7+, AutoCAD 2022 and .NET Framework 4.8.

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Jakin-huang/cad-ai-bridge/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Jakin-huang/cad-ai-bridge/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-19; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Creating simple CAD geometry from instructions and turning clean, dimensioned vector profiles into editable 3D draft parts.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-19. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source](https://github.com/rrc-ai/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md)
- **Creative results · documented:** The official README links a flange-with-four-holes video. The MCP documentation lists box, cylinder, sphere and flange primitives and describes saved FCStd/STEP plus dimension/shape checks. These are developer evidence, not a reproduced manufacturing test. [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/mcp-server/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/drawing-to-3d/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** On Windows install KIMM-CAD V26 and Python, then obtain and fully unpack the MCP/optional conversion package. Run its documented INSTALL.cmd, which creates Python dependencies and host/client connections. Restart the CAD host and configured MCP client. DWG conversion requires an additional ODA installation. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/mcp-server/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/drawing-to-3d/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** LGPL-2.1-or-later project source; KIMM-CAD/FreeCAD are separately installed. PDF processing imports AGPL-3.0/commercial PyMuPDF; DWG uses proprietary ODA File Converter with separately restricted use. Agent/model access has its own terms. Local compute plus any chosen external model or service; provider access and charges are separate. [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/mcp-server/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/drawing-to-3d/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. A single 2D drawing has no depth: thickness or axis must be supplied. Mixed multi-view sheets, scanned PDFs and extracting existing DWG 3DSOLID geometry are outside the stated converter path. Vector PDF needs scale correction and approximates circles. Shape checks and STL export do not certify printability or engineering safety.

### How it uses AI

A language-model agent can use this MCP server to construct and validate shapes in KIMM-CAD. A separate included agent skill orchestrates deterministic vector-drawing conversion to STEP/STL; the conversion arithmetic itself is not a neural model. [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/mcp-server/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/drawing-to-3d/README.md)

### Introduction

A language-model agent can use this MCP server to construct and validate shapes in KIMM-CAD. A separate included agent skill orchestrates deterministic vector-drawing conversion to STEP/STL; the conversion arithmetic itself is not a neural model. The official README links a flange-with-four-holes video. The MCP documentation lists box, cylinder, sphere and flange primitives and describes saved FCStd/STEP plus dimension/shape checks. These are developer evidence, not a reproduced manufacturing test. [Source 1](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/mcp-server/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/drawing-to-3d/README.md)

### Install

On Windows install KIMM-CAD V26 and Python, then obtain and fully unpack the MCP/optional conversion package. Run its documented INSTALL.cmd, which creates Python dependencies and host/client connections. Restart the CAD host and configured MCP client. DWG conversion requires an additional ODA installation. 1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On Windows install KIMM-CAD V26 and Python, then obtain and fully unpack the MCP/optional conversion package. Run its documented INSTALL.cmd, which creates Python dependencies and host/client connections. Restart the CAD host and configured MCP client. DWG conversion requires an additional ODA installation.
### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/NOTICE.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/mcp-server/README.md) [Source 4](https://github.com/RRC-AI/kimm-cad-mcp/blob/main/drawing-to-3d/README.md)

1. Ask the client to check cad_status and capabilities before generating geometry.
2. Create a small dimensioned box or flange, inspect the viewport and saved FCStd/STEP, and compare dimensions with the request.
3. For drawing conversion, use one clean DXF profile and supply extrusion thickness or a revolution axis; inspect the output STEP/STL and any validity diagnostics.

### Install

Install Node 22+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and Chrome or Chromium. Clone and install locked npm dependencies, run the documented doctor and render the bundled demo. Configure a Raywake account only if you want its optional AI-generation path. [Source 1](https://github.com/oguzhankayan/motion-engine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/oguzhankayan/motion-engine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Node 22+, FFmpeg/ffprobe and Chrome or Chromium. Clone and install locked npm dependencies, run the documented doctor and render the bundled demo. Configure a Raywake account only if you want its optional AI-generation path.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/oguzhankayan/motion-engine.git
```


```sh
cd motion-engine
```


```sh
npm ci
```


```sh
node bin/motion-engine.mjs doctor
```


```sh
npm run demo
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/oguzhankayan/motion-engine/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/oguzhankayan/motion-engine/blob/main/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md)

1. Inspect output/launch/film.mp4 and the generated poster, contact sheet and QA JSON.
2. Edit scene.js/config.js or choose the portrait/square starting format, then render again.
3. For AI assets, inspect the provider model schema and obtain a quote before deliberately approving paid generation; resume an uncertain job with its saved state instead of submitting another.
4. Place approved local media paths in media.json, create the film with that manifest, and review the final audio/video.

## AI Live Avatar

Avatars, digital humans & lip sync · Performance, projection & stage media · Interactive, immersive & live media

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-19; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Prototyping interactive character performances or livestreams with the creator’s own cleared avatar images and voices.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-19. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The documentation describes a public test that exposed visual consistency failures and provides a free dry-run controller. The dry run simulates moderation/queue/overlay behavior without calling Vidu; the author’s character assets and recordings are not included. [Source](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** On macOS/Linux install Python 3.11+ and FFmpeg, clone the repository and create the documented virtual environment. Install requirements, obtain the separately licensed RTC SDK using the documented vendor-fetch step, configure your own Vidu access, and start the local workbench. OBS 30+ is optional. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT controller code; Vidu, Evolink and Alibaba RTC retain proprietary service/SDK terms. Character/voice artwork and source recordings are not included or licensed by the code grant. Vidu bills real-time sessions and voice cloning; optional Evolink generation also costs separately. Check current provider rates before a test; the dry-run controller makes no Vidu call. [Source](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Background/prop editing is disabled following observed wardrobe distortion; singing, full-body performance and cross-session viewer memory are not supported. The message router is rule-based; Vidu supplies the generative AI. Generative sessions can disconnect and spend credits, and the system has not been tested here for unattended performance.

### How it uses AI

A live-production controller filters and queues audience messages, then routes them to a Vidu real-time AI avatar for speech, visual reactions and character actions. It manages wardrobe states, a local workbench and OBS overlays. [Source](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A live-production controller filters and queues audience messages, then routes them to a Vidu real-time AI avatar for speech, visual reactions and character actions. It manages wardrobe states, a local workbench and OBS overlays. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Prototyping interactive character performances or livestreams with the creator’s own cleared avatar images and voices. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The documentation describes a public test that exposed visual consistency failures and provides a free dry-run controller. The dry run simulates moderation/queue/overlay behavior without calling Vidu; the author’s character assets and recordings are not included. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

On macOS/Linux install Python 3.11+ and FFmpeg, clone the repository and create the documented virtual environment. Install requirements, obtain the separately licensed RTC SDK using the documented vendor-fetch step, configure your own Vidu access, and start the local workbench. OBS 30+ is optional. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. On macOS/Linux install Python 3.11+ and FFmpeg, clone the repository and create the documented virtual environment. Install requirements, obtain the separately licensed RTC SDK using the documented vendor-fetch step, configure your own Vidu access, and start the local workbench. OBS 30+ is optional.

```sh
git clone https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar.git
```


```sh
cd ai-live-avatar
```


```sh
python3 -m venv .venv
```


```sh
.venv/bin/pip install -r requirements.txt
```


```sh
.venv/bin/python server.py
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

1. Complete the README’s SDK and private configuration steps before starting; open http://127.0.0.1:28900/workbench.
2. Use the no-cost dry run first, then supply your own cleared character image, persona and voice for a short paid private test.
3. Keep wardrobe images consistent; test one action at a time and inspect the resulting character continuity.
4. For a performance, use the documented OBS browser-source layers and verify audio routing. Publishing or starting a public stream is a separate deliberate action.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Python 3.11+ (author used 3.13), FFmpeg with libx264/AAC, separately downloaded Alibaba RTC browser SDK, Vidu API access/credits; optional Evolink and OBS 30+.
- **Platforms:** macOS and Linux are documented. Windows support is not established. OBS and the local server must share the computer for the documented loopback browser source.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT controller code; Vidu, Evolink and Alibaba RTC retain proprietary service/SDK terms. Character/voice artwork and source recordings are not included or licensed by the code grant.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT controller code; Vidu, Evolink and Alibaba RTC retain proprietary service/SDK terms. Character/voice artwork and source recordings are not included or licensed by the code grant.
- **Cost:** Vidu bills real-time sessions and voice cloning; optional Evolink generation also costs separately. Check current provider rates before a test; the dry-run controller makes no Vidu call.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Background/prop editing is disabled following observed wardrobe distortion; singing, full-body performance and cross-session viewer memory are not supported. The message router is rule-based; Vidu supplies the generative AI. Generative sessions can disconnect and spend credits, and the system has not been tested here for unattended performance. [Source 1](https://github.com/garycaogan/ai-live-avatar/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

[Source 1](https://github.com/Navyarms/familiar/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/Navyarms/familiar/blob/main/docs/persona-pack-spec.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/Navyarms/familiar/blob/main/docs/user-guide.md)

- **Hardware:** User guide specifies 8 GB RAM minimum, 16 GB recommended and about 5 GB disk for the basic setup; CPU-tier model downloads are about 3.3 GB. A GPU is optional; larger model tiers need additional resources not quantified in this guide. Microphone required for voice input.
- **Software:** Windows app; source build uses Node 24 and uv. Local language, speech and wake-word components download separately. VRM 0.x/1.0 and the documented persona JSON/Markdown structure are supported.
- **Platforms:** Windows 10/11 64-bit is documented. macOS/Linux support is not established.

## Video Console

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-19; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Building short, shot-based videos while keeping prompts, generated versions, subtitles and final assembly in one local project.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-19. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The author documents local image and LTX image-to-video runs, including limited motion and facial deformation. These are author observations, not a reproduced quality test. The detailed GAPS register distinguishes real runs from mock-only provider checks. [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/GAPS.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** On Windows, obtain the official source ZIP and Python 3.10+, then use the documented start batch file. Alternatively the project supplies an unsigned executable package. Install FFmpeg through Settings or an existing trusted installation, and configure a separately installed ComfyUI server with the required models. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/GAPS.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT application code; ComfyUI, FFmpeg, Video2X, model weights and optional paid providers have separate terms. The optional FLUX model route has custom model terms. The author’s local Audio8Voice app is not publicly downloadable or included. Local compute/storage for local generation; separate paid cloud services if selected. Optional private Audio8Voice is unavailable to other users in the reviewed documentation. [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/GAPS.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Known gaps include preview/export differences, linked versions after splitting a shot, possible duplicate jobs after interruption, and cloud adapters tested only with mocks. LTX is capped at 257 frames (about 10.7 seconds at 24 fps), with weak motion or deformed faces in some author tests. Optional Video2X can drop two ending frames. New image templates omit model-download links. No provider availability or price claim was independently confirmed.

### How it uses AI

A Windows storyboard and timeline application routes shot prompts to ComfyUI or external generation services, tracks versions, and assembles selected images and clips into an MP4. Its ordinary editing/export tools surround the AI generation stage. [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/README.md) [Source](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/GAPS.md)

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/nice923boss/video-console/blob/main/GAPS.md)

3. Choose a paid Comfy route and review its quote, or configure your own ComfyUI server and pass its model/node check.
4. Start with a short draft and inspect identity, motion, artifacts and sound before exporting. Wan accepts one target, keeps the original background and offers 480p/720p, not the cloud route’s 1080p final.

## watch-mcp and WatchRecorder

Video, animation & film · Accessible media & assistive creation · Interactive, immersive & live media

First complete guide in this library. Documentation and complete software license checked on 2026-10-19; not a claim of a launch today.

### Quality assessment: Quality unverified

Software licensing and a concrete creative AI use are supported. The remaining checks do not establish a recommendation.

Scope: Reviewing an edit, locating a visual or timing issue, searching spoken material and communicating a recorded interactive-art workflow to an AI assistant.

Evidence checked: 2026-10-19. No local execution implied.

- **Open-source software license · verified:** Read the complete current software terms and documented notices; the full license is archived. Software licensing does not cover separate models, services or artwork. [Source](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/LICENSE)
- **Creative results · documented:** The README supplies a storyboard/assistant-response GIF and a companion-app tour. It reports timing on an M5 Pro and explicitly says other platforms and clients have not been exercised; these are author examples, not our tests. [Source](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)
- **Reproducible setup · documented:** Install Node 20+. Use the documented package installer scoped to the client you actually want, or register its MCP command manually. Download speech models with setup, inspect doctor output, then restart the client. The optional recorder is a separate macOS source build. Independent reproduction has not been established in this review. [Source](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)
- **Maintenance and support · unknown:** A release or active repository alone does not establish sustained support. Workflow-specific support and unresolved blockers need a deeper review.
- **Independent use · unknown:** Independent first-hand use or a working third-party integration was not established for this selected workflow.
- **Models, services and costs · documented:** MIT project; FFmpeg/bindings, Whisper/Silero model files and the selected AI client have separate licensing and service terms. Apple development/distribution tools are a separate host dependency. Local extraction needs no API key; the chosen AI client can have subscription or inference costs. Distribution of a separately signed Mac recorder has its own Apple requirements. [Source](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. Only local files are accepted; music and other non-speech sound are not described. Sparse sampling can miss brief actions and cursor-only motion. Local extraction does not mean end-to-end privacy: storyboard images/transcripts returned to an external AI client may leave the device. Recorder captures the whole chosen display including notifications, plus microphone, but not system audio.

### How it uses AI

An MCP server combines locally generated keyframe storyboards with Whisper speech transcription so an external multimodal AI client can inspect recordings. A Mac companion records a display and microphone into a timestamped bundle. [Source](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

An MCP server combines locally generated keyframe storyboards with Whisper speech transcription so an external multimodal AI client can inspect recordings. A Mac companion records a display and microphone into a timestamped bundle. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Reviewing an edit, locating a visual or timing issue, searching spoken material and communicating a recorded interactive-art workflow to an AI assistant. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The README supplies a storyboard/assistant-response GIF and a companion-app tour. It reports timing on an M5 Pro and explicitly says other platforms and clients have not been exercised; these are author examples, not our tests. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

Install Node 20+. Use the documented package installer scoped to the client you actually want, or register its MCP command manually. Download speech models with setup, inspect doctor output, then restart the client. The optional recorder is a separate macOS source build. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. Install Node 20+. Use the documented package installer scoped to the client you actually want, or register its MCP command manually. Download speech models with setup, inspect doctor output, then restart the client. The optional recorder is a separate macOS source build.

```sh
npx -y watch-mcp install --dry-run --client codex
```


```sh
npx -y watch-mcp install --client codex
```


```sh
npx -y watch-mcp setup
```


```sh
npx -y watch-mcp doctor
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

1. Give the configured assistant the path of a short local video and request a storyboard plus a timestamped transcript.
2. Check the source playback; use zoom on a narrow interval and frame/crop to investigate detail that sparse storyboards miss.
3. Correct transcript errors and avoid treating approximate word times as frame-accurate editing marks.
4. If using WatchRecorder, deliberately grant screen/microphone permissions and inspect the recording before handing its frames and transcript to an AI client.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Initial speech downloads are about 0.9 MB for VAD plus 574 MB on Apple Silicon or 148 MB elsewhere. Minimum RAM/VRAM and total storage are not documented. Companion recordings and cached images/transcripts add storage.
- **Software:** Node.js 20+; package supplies FFmpeg/ffprobe and prebuilt Whisper bindings. Only the local Whisper transcription backend currently exists. Optional WatchRecorder needs macOS 15+ and Swift 6 from Xcode/Command Line Tools.
- **Platforms:** Author tested macOS 26 on Apple Silicon through Claude Code. Linux, Windows, Intel Macs and other named MCP clients are anticipated but explicitly untested. WatchRecorder is Mac-only.

### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT project; FFmpeg/bindings, Whisper/Silero model files and the selected AI client have separate licensing and service terms. Apple development/distribution tools are a separate host dependency.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT project; FFmpeg/bindings, Whisper/Silero model files and the selected AI client have separate licensing and service terms. Apple development/distribution tools are a separate host dependency.
- **Cost:** Local extraction needs no API key; the chosen AI client can have subscription or inference costs. Distribution of a separately signed Mac recorder has its own Apple requirements.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Limitations

Only local files are accepted; music and other non-speech sound are not described. Sparse sampling can miss brief actions and cursor-only motion. Local extraction does not mean end-to-end privacy: storyboard images/transcripts returned to an external AI client may leave the device. Recorder captures the whole chosen display including notifications, plus microphone, but not system audio. [Source 1](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### Get the tool

- [Repository](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp)
- [Documentation](https://github.com/Takinggg/watch-mcp/blob/main/README.md)

### How it uses AI

A ComfyUI model patch changes MOSS-Image positional-frequency scaling and its sampling schedule to explore higher-resolution image generation without retraining the model. [Source](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

### Introduction

A ComfyUI model patch changes MOSS-Image positional-frequency scaling and its sampling schedule to explore higher-resolution image generation without retraining the model. [Source 1](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

### What it is good for

Comparing high-resolution MOSS renders and reducing aspect-ratio distortion while retaining an editable ComfyUI workflow. [Source 1](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

### Demo & examples

The repository supplies a 2560-square, eight-step workflow using a DMD LoRA and an author geometry comparison. Reported speed overhead and quality improvements were not independently reproduced. [Source 1](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

### Install

In an existing compatible ComfyUI installation, install the registered custom node or clone the official repository into custom_nodes, then restart. Obtain the separately licensed MOSS model and example workflow’s required LoRA before rendering. [Source 1](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

1. Obtain the official source or release linked below.
2. In an existing compatible ComfyUI installation, install the registered custom node or clone the official repository into custom_nodes, then restart. Obtain the separately licensed MOSS model and example workflow’s required LoRA before rendering.

```sh
comfy node install comfyui-mossdype
```

### First project

Start with a short, representative project and inspect the result before a larger batch. [Source 1](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

1. Load example_workflows/mossdype_ultrares.json and resolve the required checkpoint/LoRA filenames.
2. Locate MOSS-Image DyPE-M Patch under model/patch/moss_image and connect MODEL input/output in the sampling path.
3. Compare a fixed-seed baseline with the patch enabled; keep isotropic scaling and start from a modest target_scale such as the author’s 1.2–1.3 range.
4. Inspect faces, text and geometry at full size; use the Schedule node to inspect settings without rendering and reduce strength if texture is invented.
### Hardware & software

Requirements below are documented for this workflow. Unspecified limits are not inferred. [Source 1](https://github.com/IxMxAMAR/comfyui-mossdype/blob/main/README.md)

- **Hardware:** Minimum system RAM, VRAM and total disk space are not documented in the reviewed instructions.
- **Software:** Compatible ComfyUI and MOSS-Image model; the custom node states no additional Python dependencies beyond ComfyUI. The supplied example also uses a DMD LoRA.
- **Platforms:** Runs inside a suitable ComfyUI environment. The repository does not publish a tested OS/GPU matrix; host support is not proof this particular workflow was tested on every platform.

- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT custom-node source. ComfyUI, MOSS checkpoints, LoRA files and source images have their own grants; the patch does not change them.

Documentation review only; AI Media Scout did not install or run this software. The CLI currently hardcodes CPU int8 transcription with four threads. ### How it uses AI

A Python pipeline uses Faster-Whisper and voice-activity detection to transcribe video, then translates and rewrites Persian subtitle text and exports timed subtitles or burned-in video. ### License, model weights & costs

The full software license was reviewed. Model weights, assets and services remain separate. [Source 1](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/LICENSE) [Source 2](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 3](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/subtitle_engine.py) [Source 4](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/requirements.txt)

- **Code:** MIT
- **Weights:** MIT application source. Whisper model, dependencies and font retain separate terms. Google translation is an external service, with its own access and data-handling terms; the MIT grant provides no service entitlement.
- **Commercial:** Software use follows MIT. MIT application source. Whisper model, dependencies and font retain separate terms. Google translation is an external service, with its own access and data-handling terms; the MIT grant provides no service entitlement.
- **Cost:** Local compute/storage and network translation. GitHub Actions usage and any external service limits/charges are separate; no guaranteed free processing allowance is established.

### Why it merits attention

Included for the documented creative workflow. A complete guide and popularity do not establish output reliability. [Source 1](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/subtitle_engine.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Limitations

The CLI currently hardcodes CPU int8 transcription with four threads. Its gemini-key argument is not used by the inspected translation function. Translation errors can fall back to source text processed by Persian rewrite rules, so inspect every line. The unauthenticated translation endpoint is not a guaranteed supported service API. The optional GitHub workflow advertises outputs in Releases, which can expose media publicly. [Source 1](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/README.md) [Source 2](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/subtitle_engine.py) [Source 3](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/requirements.txt)

### Get the tool

- [Documentation](https://github.com/voughtboysjj-rgb/AI-Subtitle-Generator/blob/main/README.md)
